Let me close with the story of Dame Fina Cooper because a lot of times we talk about the personal thing, what should this Uber driver do? But then there are other times we talk about systemic injustice. Are we all victims? So Dame Cooper was an indigenous person growing up in New Zealand. And New Zealand was the last major country to be colonized by the United Kingdom.  And so huge amounts of Native lands were taken, and the Indigenous people were disenfranchised in a lot of ways. It's really tragic when you think about it.  And Dame Cooper, at the age of 78 or so, decided that she would use her status and affiliation within the tribe she was part of to capture the attention of the entire nation and try to put Indigenous people's rights on the agenda. So you look at this and say, here's this woman. She's not famous. She doesn't have a lot of money. She's almost 80 years old. What could she possibly do?  This is a problem or it's a situation. If it's a situation, what are we going to do? Just live with it? But if it's a problem, let's figure out where are the pieces to make progress happen. She organizes 50 people. Most of us could organize 50 people and they start marching to the Capitol.  And they do the work to spread the news in advance. And day after day after day for more than a month, this group of people is walking to the Capitol. She's on the front page of every newspaper in New Zealand by halfway through.  Now there's 100 people, 200 people, 2,000 people. By the time they get to the Capitol, there are 4,000 people patiently marching side by side. And they presented their demands, requirements, suggestions to the parliament and...  Too late, not enough, but in a very big way, it shifted the culture of the entire country, changed the laws about how land was allocated, and made a really fundamental difference. She didn't do that by herself.  But she understood she had enough agency to connect the others, to create a culture, to show up and show up and show up with a practice. So if one 80-year-old woman can change an entire country, what can we do?  We can't drive to London or Washington DC and change everything. But what we can do is find the others, come up with the story we can tell ourselves and the others and establish a new normal. The only way the world has ever changed is when a few people come together, build a culture and repeat it over and over and over again.
